ParlaMint-HR_2022-04-07-0.u102340	Thank you very much, Mr. President. On 4 March 2022, the honourable parliamentary representatives of the Council of the EU adopted an implementing decision establishing the existence of a mass inflow of displaced persons from Ukraine within the meaning of Article 5(5). Directive 2001/55/EC which has the effect of introducing temporary protection. The present Decision introduced temporary protection for Ukrainian citizens who were resident in Ukraine and who were displaced on 24 February '22. year or after that date because of a military invasion of the Russian armed forces that began that day. In view of the exceptional and exceptional nature of the situation, including the military invasion of the Russian Federation in Ukraine and the proportion of mass inflows of displaced persons, temporary protection should allow those persons to enjoy harmonised rights across the EU that offer them an adequate level of protection including health care. This legislative proposal regulates that persons under temporary protection exercise the right to health care within the same scope as insured persons from compulsory health insurance, as well as asylum seekers and foreigners under subsidiary protection. I would also like to point out that the government has established an immediate inter-resor working group and as well as an expert group of crisis headquarters of the Ministry of Health which we immediately responded, gave all the necessary instructions on the ground and so far more than 4,000 healthcare services have already been done for the displaced citizens of Ukraine. Other categories of users The Social Care Act also identify categories of foreigners who can recognise rights or grant social services in the social care system, which are foreigners with permanent residence and long-term residence in the Republic of Croatia, persons without citizenship with temporary and permanent residence in the Republic of Croatia, foreigners under subsidiary protection and asylum, and members of their family, foreigners with established status of victims of trafficking. Russian military aggression and invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, a large number of displaced people from Ukraine and members of their family found shelter and security outside Ukraine's borders, and a large number in the Republic of Croatia. The decision of the Government of the Republic of Croatia has introduced temporary protection for displaced persons from Ukraine in accordance with the Law on International and Temporary Protection and Implementing Decision of the Council of the EU of 4 March 2022 establishing the existence of a mass inflow of displaced persons from Ukraine. Under the applicable law, a foreigner under temporary protection has the right to a one-time fee and the right to an accommodation service under the terms of the prescribed law and, if required by the living circumstances in which he was found. This legislative proposal extends the scope of compensation and services for foreigners under temporary protection in such a way as to ensure that the compensation and services provided by the parties under subsidiary protection and asylum in order to enable displaced persons from Ukraine to meet the requirements laid down in the social care system. The following is proposed by the Croatian Parliament to adopt the Law amending and supplementing the Social Care Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2022-04-07-0.u102540	Thank you very much, Mr. President. On 4 March 2022, the honourable parliamentary representatives of the Council of the EU adopted an implementing decision establishing the existence of a mass inflow of displaced persons from Ukraine within the meaning of Article 5(5). Directive 2001/55/EC which has the effect of introducing temporary protection. The present Decision introduced temporary protection for Ukrainian citizens who were resident in Ukraine and who were displaced on 24 February '22. year or after that date because of a military invasion of the Russian armed forces that began that day. In view of the exceptional and exceptional nature of the situation, including the military invasion of the Russian Federation in Ukraine and the proportion of mass inflows of displaced persons, temporary protection should allow those persons to enjoy harmonised rights across the EU that offer them an adequate level of protection including health care. This legislative proposal regulates that persons under temporary protection exercise the right to health care within the same scope as insured persons from compulsory health insurance, as well as asylum seekers and foreigners under subsidiary protection. I would also like to point out that the government has established an immediate inter-resor working group and as well as an expert group of crisis headquarters of the Ministry of Health which we immediately responded, gave all the necessary instructions on the ground and so far more than 4,000 healthcare services have already been done for the displaced citizens of Ukraine. We also accept, I accept the no-technical amendment of the Legislative Committee. We are proposing the adoption of this legislative proposal, following all of these. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2022-04-07-0.u102542	Thank you very much. Dear President, ladies and gentlemen, the Law on Social Welfare is a fundamental regulation governing the activity of social care, users, rights and services and procedures for achieving them as well as other issues relevant to the activity of social care. Other categories of users The Social Care Act also identify categories of foreigners who can recognise rights or grant social services in the social care system, which are foreigners with permanent residence and long-term residence in the Republic of Croatia, persons without citizenship with temporary and permanent residence in the Republic of Croatia, foreigners under subsidiary protection and asylum, and members of their family, foreigners with established status of victims of trafficking. Russian military aggression and invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, a large number of displaced people from Ukraine and members of their family found shelter and security outside Ukraine's borders, and a large number in the Republic of Croatia. The decision of the Government of the Republic of Croatia has introduced temporary protection for displaced persons from Ukraine in accordance with the Law on International and Temporary Protection and Implementing Decision of the Council of the EU of 4 March 2022 establishing the existence of a mass inflow of displaced persons from Ukraine. Under the applicable law, a foreigner under temporary protection has the right to a one-time fee and the right to an accommodation service under the terms of the prescribed law and, if required by the living circumstances in which he was found. This legislative proposal extends the scope of compensation and services for foreigners under temporary protection in such a way as to ensure that the compensation and services provided by the parties under subsidiary protection and asylum in order to enable displaced persons from Ukraine to meet the requirements laid down in the social care system. The following is proposed by the Croatian Parliament to adopt the Law amending and supplementing the Social Care Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2022-04-07-0.u83541	Thank you, Vice President. Dear colleagues and colleagues, today I will talk about a package of measures to mitigate energy prices that the government adopted in mid-February. The package of measures is in effect on 1 April, the total value of 4.8 billion kuna, and the state will thus directly assist citizens and the economy in view of the expected increase in energy prices this month. The first measure is to lower the VAT rate. The parliamentary procedure adopted changes to the VAT law which reduces the value added tax rate from 25 to 13 and to 5% and from 13 to 5% to several categories of products and services. Then grant gas support to households and entrepreneurs. The government will subsidise a part of the gas price to households, directly in the account of 10 lip support per kilowatt hour. This amount of support will be shown on the account, and suppliers will send a fee claim to the Ministry of Economy that will settle it every month from April 2022 to April 2023. In order to mitigate energy prices, the Ministry of Economy on Sustainable Development in cooperation with HAMAG BICR has launched a programme to grant support to reduce gas costs for micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. It is a small-value support for the reduction of the gas consumption account of 15 limes per kilowatt of spent gas to entrepreneurs with annual gas consumption of up to 10 gigawatts of hours. Entrepreneurs can apply for support through an online application available on the HAMAG BICRO website. The application procedure is simple, fast fully digitized. The decision to grant support by email shall be given to the entrepreneur and the gas supplier shall be provided with a voucher from which the monthly subsidized amount will automatically be refused with the March 2023 account. Three measures are also in place to reduce the impact of energy price increases on socially endangered groups of citizens of the Republic of Croatia. This is an increase in the scope and level of compensation for the threatened buyer of energy, an extended definition of threatened customers of energy, and are with existing users, and these were users of minimum remuneration guaranteed and users of personal disability included users of national compensation for older people, beneficiaries of cash compensation for unemployed Croatian defenders and beneficiaries of financial compensation for civilian victims of the Homeland War. The monthly amount of compensation for the threatened buyer of energy from 200 to 400 kuna for the next year has also been increased and its intention, purpose for gas or thermal energy has been expanded rather than just to date on electricity alone. The Centre for Social Care shall adopt a decision on the recognition of the right to compensation for the threatened buyer of energy. The following measures are compensation for social service providers relating to legal persons who are 1,100 and who care for 40,000 users. They will receive a monthly fee from 1,000 to 4,000 kuna depending on the number of users they provide services to. On 6 April, the Ministry of Labour, Pension System, Family and Social Policy issued a public call which was open until 22 April at 15 hours and which can be contacted by accommodation service providers, organised housing, stay and help in the house of preparation and delivery of meals. Application forms can be downloaded and submitted to the Ministry's website. Following the examination of the dossier, a decision on financing will also be made public on the web pages. The fee is also available to foster families, a monthly fee of 400 kuna for foster families. Hosts shall not be required to make a public call for them to decide on financing the basis of data from the host register. Then there's an energy supplement for the pensioners. The government of the Republic of Croatia has made a decision on the payment of a one-time monetary allowance to pensioners modelled on a Covid supplement that will ease the impact on energy prices and facilitate the situation of pensioners. The energy supplement refers to pensioners with a pension of up to 4,000 kuna and the amount of 400 to 1,200 kuna will depend on the amount of pension. And this way I call on all potential support users to use their right to use the funds that the Government of the Republic of Croatia has intended to mitigate the impact of increasing energy prices on the standard of citizens and entrepreneurs. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2022-04-07-0.u83622	Dear Vice President, colleagues and colleagues, I would like to point out today a very important topic, which is the problem of emergency medical staff. For a while they heard sirens from the ambulance every Monday at exactly noon throughout Croatia, not as a sign of emergency intervention, but as a sign for everyone else to hear their cries and their requests, because in short, the ambulance needs help. For the past few months, emergency medical workers have been loudly and publicly seeking the right to an underprivileged job internship. They light rotational lights, publish disturbing photographs of a car full of blood and bandages, convince the unloved and distressing scenes with interventions, address the authorities all to point to the enormous amount of stress and the importance of the work they do. They talk about stressful situations faced daily from severe traffic accidents to resuscitation of children from life-threatening injuries, heart attacks, strokes. So, all those situations where every second is crucial. These people deal with an unimaginable dose of stress every day that some of us can't even imagine. Every day of their work is unpredictable and dynamic and they literally have to give 100 percent of themselves not only physically, but also mentally. Because as we know ourselves in situations where someone needs medical attention every second plays a role. And they're not really looking for anything unrealistic or anything impossible. They are looking for a well-favoured work experience that they are certainly entitled to in view of the weight of their work as well as the difficult working conditions. It is a job in which, for example, it is figuratively spent both physically and mentally, and therefore it is illusory to expect that they can have the same reaction and equal speed with, for example, 60 years of life. In addition, there is also equipment that often has to be carried on several floors, and quickly because in these situations every second is crucial. As they say themselves, these are stressful situations that leave consequences, and it is very difficult to separate business from private ones in such a workplace, because some scenes do not remain carved just that day, but remain etched for life. And that is why many health ministers spoke to Beroshu, director of the Croatian Emergency Medicine Institute Grba-Bujević as well as colleagues. They also announced a new protest outside Mark's square next Thursday (April 14th). Although both the Minister of Health and the Director of the Croatian Emergency Medicine Institute support such requests and support them, the ball is transferred from one to the other, to the union, to employers or County Emergency Medicine Institute, and what is the most important solution for them is still missing. In order for the situation to be even weirder, they had the right to an underprivileged internship but it was taken away from them at the end of the 1990s and it was another absurdity in this country. The sound of an ambulance siren that has a lot of us lurking blood in our veins and causing them discomfort is everyday. Therefore, from this pay phone and on behalf of the IDS, but I would like to personally show my full support to emergency medical staff and appeal to the authorities to give them the right to what they definitely deserve with their work, and this is a benefited work experience because they are the only emergency service in Croatia that has no right and that would be said smart enough in the people.
